## AGY-1  (WS-DEPLOY, P1) — atomic+retried+verified for the remaining firstboot producers  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash/systemd). **When:** first — it mirrors the webtools/venv fixes Claude already landed.
**What + How:** apply the 38-hermes-agent reference pattern to the producers that still "run once, give up":
- `usr/libexec/mios/mios-ai-firstboot` — the vLLM/GGUF weights fetch (the `snapshot_download` blocks, ~L111-124 + L371): wrap in a 3x retry-with-backoff loop, verify the target `config.json`/`.ready` exists after, non-fatal per-model, log clearly. (This is the code half of **T-178**; the heavy lane is skipped today purely because this fetch had no retry and ran through the then-missing venv.)
- `usr/libexec/mios/forge-firstboot.sh` — replace the fixed `300s` timeout abort with a **readiness poll** (poll the forge health/socket, generous cap, `Restart=on-failure` on the unit) so a slow forge under load never hard-fails.
- Any other `*-firstboot` producer with a bare one-shot build/fetch (grep `usr/libexec/mios/*firstboot*`).
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/mios-ai-firstboot`, `usr/libexec/mios/forge-firstboot.sh`, their `usr/lib/systemd/system/*.service` (`Restart=on-failure` + `StartLimit*`).
**Done When:** each producer is atomic+retried+verified+idempotent; `bash -n` clean; a fresh reinstall would deploy them without manual retries.

## AGY-5  (WS-GUARD / T-173, **P0**) — daemon runaway controls (host-pressure gate + dedup + cron cap)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python, agent-pipe/daemon). **When:** next — P0, prevents GPU/host runaway.
**What + How:** add guardrails to the consolidated micro-LLM daemon + agent-pipe so autonomous loops can't starve the host: (1) a **host-pressure gate** — before a heavy dispatch, check GPU VRAM / CPU load and defer/degrade to the light lane when over a threshold (from `[ai.host_thresholds]`); (2) **request dedup** — collapse identical in-flight prompts (hash the normalized messages) so a retry storm doesn't fan out N copies; (3) a **cron cap** — bound the daemon's scheduled classify/refusal jobs per interval. Degrade-open (missing signal → allow).
**Where:** `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/**` (daemon/dispatch), `usr/libexec/mios/mios-daemon*`, `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (`[ai.host_thresholds]`, a new `[ai.guard]` if needed).
**Done When:** a synthetic runaway (rapid identical heavy prompts under high VRAM) is gated/deduped, not fanned out; `test_mios_*` green.

## AGY-9  (WS-VECTOR V1 / T-243, **P1**) — config read-path resolver (kill the write-only `system_config` drift)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python). **When:** next — it's the payoff of your AGY-3 foundation (the tables exist and round-trip; now make something READ them).
**What + How:** write a DB config resolver that is a **peer of `mios_toml.py`**, reading `config_kv` / `verb` / `domain_verb` (the tables AGY-3 + `seed-db-config.py` populate) with the **baked `mios.toml` as fail-open fallback**. Contract: same 3-layer precedence semantics as the TOML overlay (vendor<host<user<machine via the `config_layer` rank), same return shapes the current `mios_toml.py` callers expect — a drop-in so a caller can flip its source with no behavior change. Add a **`[ai] db_authoritative` sentinel** (default **false**): when false, resolve from TOML exactly as today and only *shadow-compare* the DB answer (log divergence, count it); when true, resolve from DB with TOML fallback. This lets us flip authority per-surface later (V5) with the gate already watching. Do NOT flip any real caller yet — ship the resolver + shadow-compare + tests only.
**Where:** new `usr/lib/mios/mios_db_config.py` (peer of `usr/lib/mios/mios_toml.py`), `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (`[ai] db_authoritative=false`), a `test_mios_db_config.py` under `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/` (or `tests/`).
**Done When:** resolver returns identical values to `mios_toml.py` for a sampled set of keys/verbs (shadow-compare = 0 divergences on the current tree); TOML fail-open verified by pointing at an empty DB; `bash -n`/parse clean; tests green; `just drift-gate` passes. **Storage law:** vectors find, SQL reads — resolve from typed columns, never from `emb`.

## AGY-10  (WS-VECTOR V2 backfill / T-244, **P1**) — embed-backfill worker that actually FILLS your AGY-7 columns  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python). **When:** after AGY-7 (you added the `emb` columns; nothing populates them yet — close the loop).
**What + How:** extend `mios_pipe/memory/embed_backfill.py` (or add a worker beside it) to populate the `emb vector(768)` columns AGY-7 added on `skill`/`verb`/`tool_call`/`directory_entry` by computing embeddings over each row's **TEXT PROJECTION** via `mios-llm-light /v1/embeddings` (`nomic-embed-text`, the same client seam `pg.py` uses). Must be: **idempotent** (only (re)embed rows whose `emb IS NULL` or whose `emb_version` is stale — never re-embed unchanged rows), **provenance-stamped** (`emb_model`/`emb_version` written with each vector), **batched with the 3x-retry-with-backoff pattern** (reference `38-hermes-agent.sh`), and **fail-open** (embeddings endpoint down → log + skip, never crash the caller). Then repoint the in-process verb/apps embedding rebuild (`worker_tools.py`) to prefer a native `verb.emb <=> query` lookup when populated, with the in-process lexicon as the fail-open fallback (this is the retirement AGY-7 set up).
**Where:** `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/memory/embed_backfill.py`, `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/routing/worker_tools.py`, a `test_mios_backfill.py`.
**Done When:** running the backfill on a seeded DB fills `verb.emb`/`skill.emb`/`tool_call.emb`/`directory_entry.emb` with provenance; a second run is a near-noop (idempotent); `<=>` verb search returns sane neighbors; endpoint-down path degrades to lexicon with no crash; tests green.

**Who:** you (Python). **When:** after AGY-14 (needs the shadow-compare); builds on your AGY-9 resolver.

When the sentinel is **false** (default): keep resolving the verb catalog + config from `mios.toml` exactly as today, but ALSO shadow-read the DB and record divergences (reuse AGY-14's telemetry). When **true**: resolve the verb catalog + config from the DB (`verb`/`domain_verb`/`config_kv`), TOML fail-open. ## AGY-25  (AUDIT FIXES, **P0/P1**) — resolve 10 defects a Claude adversarial audit CONFIRMED in your WS-VECTOR code  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python + bash + SQL). **When:** NEXT — these are real, verified bugs in code you shipped (each independently code-traced + verified). Claude already fixed 2 (redact.py unquoted-secret leak + mios_db_config.py port fail-open) — do NOT redo those. Fix these 10:
**HIGH:**
1. **`verbcatalog.py` ~148-215 — the `db_authoritative` sentinel is a NO-OP.** The T-126 "Database Overlay" block runs UNCONDITIONALLY (only try/except-guarded), so with the sentinel FALSE (default) + pgvector up, the DB silently overlays/deletes verbs (`cat.pop` on is_active=false, overwrites sig/desc/tier/permission/cmd/params) — the returned catalog is NOT byte-identical to TOML. **Fix:** gate the entire T-126 overlay behind `is_db_authoritative()` so sentinel-FALSE returns the pure TOML catalog; keep only the shadow-compare (read-only) running when false.
2. **`embed_backfill.py` ~209-248 + `toolsearch.py` ~206-231 — emb_version PING-PONG on `verb`.** Backfill stamps `emb_version='nomic-768-v1'`; toolsearch treats a verb vector valid only if `emb_version == <sha256 fp>` and re-stamps fp. The two writers perpetually invalidate each other -> every core/common verb re-embeds every 15-min timer run (never idempotent). **Fix:** unify to ONE scheme on `verb.emb_version` (make backfill compute+store the SAME fingerprint toolsearch uses, OR make toolsearch honor backfill's version) so a stamped vector satisfies both readers.
3. **`agent_call.py` ~93/436/543 — token guard permanently WEDGES a session.** `_SESSION_TOKENS[session_id]` is cumulative-only, never windowed/reset; once a session crosses `conversation_token_ceil` (2M) `used > conv_ceil` raises `ValueError` on EVERY later dispatch for the process lifetime -> the user can never get another answer until daemon restart. **Fix:** make it a rolling window + graceful summarize (mirror `chat.py`'s `_budget_admit`/`_BUDGET_LEDGER`), not a permanent hard-raise.
**MEDIUM:**
4. **`mios_db_config.py` ~13/197 — `DIVERGENCES` counts read-observations, never resets** -> the health `config_divergences` gauge climbs unbounded on any persistent mismatch read on hot paths. **Fix:** track a SET of distinct divergent `scope.key`s (or reset per rebuild) so the gauge = number of divergent settings.
5. **`verbcatalog.py` ~234 — shadow-compare feeds the ALREADY-DB-OVERLAID `cat`** into `_compare_catalogs`, masking real TOML-vs-DB drift on shared verbs AND firing a false divergence for every TOML-only verb during partial migration. **Fix:** snapshot a PRE-overlay pure-TOML copy and compare THAT vs `db_cat`.
**LOW:**
6. `verbcatalog.py` ~225 — redundant SECOND blocking `psycopg.connect(connect_timeout=2)` on the sentinel-FALSE path (~4s dead wait when DB down). Skip/cache when not db_authoritative.
7. `toolsearch.py` ~231 — stamps `emb_model='qwen2.5-coder:1.5b'` on nomic-produced verb vectors (false provenance, disagrees with backfill's `nomic-embed-text`). Stamp the actual embed model.
8. `embed_backfill.py` ~243-249 — `embedded_count += 1` runs even when the degrade-open `mios_pg.execute()` UPDATE returned None (silent DB failure over-reported as success). Check the return; count only real writes.
9. `automation/38-drift-checks.sh` check 32 (~2707-2770) — "lossless" round-trip only diffs pkgs + phase ordinal/deps/stage; add `enable`/`layer`/`base_image_ref`/`section` + `debloat_profile.description` + `preset.debloat_profile_name` to the diff.
10. `materialize-build-ctx.py` ~59 — same-ordinal `build_phase` tie-breaks by DB `id`, so an incremental re-seed sorts a new same-prefix script after older peers instead of lexically. **Fix:** `ORDER BY stage, ordinal NULLS LAST, script` (lexical), not `id`.
**Where/Done When:** fix each at the cited file:line; add/extend tests (`test_mios_db_config`/`test_mios_backfill`/`test_mios_verbcatalog`) to cover the fixed behavior; `just drift-gate` green; **stage only your files (never the Quadlets).**

## AGY-27  (embedding hygiene / research-gap #6+#7, **P1**) — fix the emb_version space-collision + add EmbeddingGemma task prefixes  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python + SQL). **When:** NEXT after AGY-25 — this OVERLAPS AGY-25 finding #2 (unify the emb_version scheme first). Source: gaps 6+7.
**What + How:** two real embedding-correctness bugs: (a) the served embedding model was swapped **nomic-embed-text → EmbeddingGemma-300m under the SAME served name AND same emb_version**, so two incompatible 768-d vector spaces now collide in one namespace — **bump the emb_version** (a new fingerprint that includes the actual model+revision) so old-space vectors are treated stale and re-embedded, and make backfill + toolsearch agree on that ONE fingerprint (this IS the AGY-25 #2 unification — do them together). (b) EmbeddingGemma REQUIRES task prompt templates (`task: search result | query: …` / document prefixes) — the embed call path (`server.py` embed seam + `embed_backfill.py`) sends **raw text**, degrading recall. Add the EmbeddingGemma query/document prefixes at the embed seam (gated by the active embed model so nomic-only setups are unaffected). Then trigger a one-time full re-embed via the backfill worker.
**Where:** `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/memory/embed_backfill.py` + `routing/toolsearch.py` (emb_version unify), the embed seam in `server.py`, `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (embed model/version), `test_mios_backfill.py`.
**Done When:** old-space vectors are invalidated + re-embedded once (not ping-ponging — see AGY-25 #2); EmbeddingGemma requests carry the task prefixes; nomic path unchanged; recall verified sane; tests + `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-28  (AGY-25 RESIDUALS, **P1**) — close the 4 residual defects a Claude verification pass found still open after AGY-25  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python). **When:** NEXT. A Claude adversarial re-verify confirmed AGY-25 landed 10/10 but left these tails:
1. **`agent_call.py` — D3 still HARD-RAISES.** The rolling-window is in (good, permanent-wedge gone) but once the 1h-windowed total exceeds `conversation_token_ceil` it still raises `ValueError` (500-style) instead of the **graceful summarize** the defect asked for. **Fix:** mirror `chat.py`'s `_budget_admit` degrade — summarize/trim + admit, never a hard raise on a live session.
2. **`mios_db_config.py` + `verbcatalog.py:~246` — D4 not airtight.** `get_divergences()` sums the deduped `_DIVERGENT_KEYS` set PLUS the raw int `DIVERGENCES`, and `verbcatalog.py` still does `mios_db_config.DIVERGENCES += 1` per divergent reload. **Fix:** route the verbcatalog contributor into the SAME `_DIVERGENT_KEYS` set (scope.key granularity) and drop the raw-int path so the gauge = distinct divergent settings.
3. **`embed_backfill.py` — D2 co-residency assumption.** `_verb_embed_fingerprint()` reads toolsearch's module-global `_VERB_CATALOG`; if the 15-min worker runs in a process where `configure()` never populated it, the fp is computed over an empty catalog → churn returns. **Fix:** guard/assert the catalog is populated before fingerprinting (skip the run + log if empty), or compute the fp from the same SSOT source both processes read.
4. **`verbcatalog.py` — D6 first-load blocking connect.** On the sentinel-FALSE path the FIRST load still does one blocking `psycopg.connect(connect_timeout=2)` before `_DB_UNREACHABLE` caches. **Fix:** short-circuit the very first probe too (cache the unreachable state across the initial call, or make the probe non-blocking/background).
**Done When:** each fixed at cited file:line; tests extended; `just drift-gate` green; stage only your files.

## AGY-33  (audit-redaction depth / follow AGY-32, **P2**) — make config_event redaction VALUE-aware, not just scope/key-aware  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (SQL + Python test). **When:** after AGY-32. The Day-0 readiness audit found a coverage gap in the `mios_redact_config_value()` fix (Claude commit 3abc92f): `seed-db-config.py` §1c seeds the `[containers]` scope into config_kv, whose Environment arrays embed secrets — `K3S_TOKEN=...` (mios.toml:10004), `WEBUI_SECRET_KEY=...` (:10183), `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`/`SUDO_PASSWORD`/`PASSWORD` (9717-18, 10264). The trigger only redacts when `scope IN ('security','agent_passport')` OR the IMMEDIATE key matches the secret regex; `scope='containers', key='mios-k3s'` matches neither, so the whole container JSON (secrets included) lands in config_event verbatim. Same structural miss for any secret nested in a subtable of a non-secret scope.
**How (pick one, document why):** (a) make `mios_redact_config_value` recurse the JSONB value and mask (i) any object key matching the secret regex and (ii) any string of the `NAME=VALUE` env-array shape where NAME matches the regex; OR (b) exclude `scope IN ('containers','image')` from the config_kv seed loop in seed-db-config.py (they're Quadlet-shaped, not operator config — also trims audit noise). **Who:** you (Python, agent-pipe). **When:** now or right after AGY-47 (server.py decomposition) — this adds two routes + a config writer. Claude is landing the CLIENT half in `usr/share/mios/configurator/mios.html` (inert/degrade-to-download until these routes exist), so ship these and the loop closes.
**What + How:** the Portal already embeds the configurator (`GET /portal/configurator` serves mios.html into the shell iframe), but mios.html can only save via browser download — there is NO server read/write of the SSOT. Add two routes:
- **`GET /portal/config`** → return the live LAYERED `mios.toml` (vendor<host<user) as `text/plain` TOML for the form to seed from. Reuse the existing read path (the `tomllib.load` the Portal already does for `[colors]`/`[ports]`, `mios_pipe/kernel/config.py`).
- **`POST /portal/config`** → body = raw TOML; validate it parses; PERSIST to the **user layer `~/.config/mios/mios.toml`** by default (host `/etc/mios/mios.toml` only when the session is a privileged/admin operator). Return a JSON status. **Persist-only — do NOT trigger `mios build`** (apply stays an explicit operator action).
- Add the **writer** to `mios_pipe/kernel/config.py` (currently read-only): write the correct layer file atomically (temp+rename), preserving SSOT cascade semantics.
- **Auth:** reuse the portal session (`_portal_authed`) + honor `[security].api_require_auth`; when unauthorized/disabled return 403/404 so the client gracefully falls back to browser-download (the client half is built to degrade).
- Wire the routes in `mios_pipe/routing/portal.py` + the thin `@app`/router entry in `server.py` (coordinate with your AGY-47 decomposition).
**Where:** `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/kernel/config.py` (writer), `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/routing/portal.py` (routes), `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/server.py` (wiring), a `test_mios_config_write.py`.
**Done When:** `GET /portal/config` returns the live TOML; `POST /portal/config` persists to `~/.config/mios/mios.toml` atomically, auth-gated, no build triggered; unauthorized → 403 so the client degrades to download; tests green; `just drift-gate` green. Stage only your files.
**OPEN (operator may adjust):** the write-layer default (per-user vs host), and whether a future 'Apply' action should trigger `mios build` (out of scope here — persist-only for now).

## AGY-55  (WS-CONFIG / T-267 hardening, **P2**) — test + refactor the /portal/config endpoints AGY-50 shipped  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python, agent-pipe). **When:** after AGY-50 (done + pushed) — polish it. Claude's carve-out `usr/share/mios/configurator/**` still applies (the HTML client is Claude's), but the agent-pipe routes are yours.
**What + How:** AGY-50 landed `GET`/`POST /portal/config` (functional — auth-gated, validates TOML, atomic temp+rename write to `mios_toml.USER`) but with gaps to close:
1. **No dedicated test.** Add `test_mios_config_write.py` covering: `GET /portal/config` returns the layered TOML; `POST` persists to the USER layer (mock `mios_toml.USER` to a temp path, assert the file content round-trips); `POST` of invalid TOML → 400; both endpoints unauthenticated → 401.
2. **Duplicated hand-rolled serializer.** `dict_to_toml` is copy-pasted in BOTH the GET (portal.py ~1584) and POST (~1666) handlers. Extract ONE `to_toml()` serializer + the USER-layer atomic writer into `mios_pipe/kernel/config.py` (the config kernel — this is where the original design put the writer) and call them from both handlers. Verify the serializer round-trips the scalar/section/array shapes the configurator form uses (the `[data-key]` inputs).
3. **Synchronous DB-reseed on POST.** The POST triggers a `seed-db-config` subprocess synchronously after the write — make it NON-BLOCKING (FastAPI `BackgroundTask` / fire-and-forget) and degrade-open (a reseed failure must NOT fail the POST — the file write already succeeded), so a slow reseed never hangs the operator's Save.
**Where:** new `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/test_mios_config_write.py`, `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/kernel/config.py` (extracted `to_toml` + writer), `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/routing/portal.py` (call the extracted helpers; make reseed background).
**Done When:** the endpoint test passes (GET / POST / invalid-400 / unauth-401); one `to_toml`+writer in `config.py` used by both handlers; the DB-reseed is background + degrade-open; `just drift-gate` green. Stage only your files.

## AGY-57..58 — ADR-0010 completion (from a Claude design workflow: extend mios-theme-render IN PLACE, NO mios-dotfiles-render fork)  **[DONE]**

> These implement the `kind` axis + merge modes for the dotfiles engine Claude built (`_load_surfaces`/`_KINDS`/cmd_render/cmd_check/cmd_apply in `usr/libexec/mios/mios-theme-render`). CORE RULE: `kind` is WRITE-SEMANTICS dispatch, ORTHOGONAL to the existing `section` (token-source). ABSENT `kind` ⇒ `template` ⇒ the 8 existing surfaces need ZERO edits + stay BYTE-IDENTICAL. Preserve every fail-loud backstop (unknown kind ⇒ `exit 3`; a merge surface missing its fixture ⇒ `exit 3`). `capture` stays template-only.

## AGY-57  (WS-DOTFILES / ADR-0010, **P2**) — the `kind` axis + `json-merge` mode (Windows Terminal / VS Code settings.json)  **[DONE]**
> **[DONE by Claude on main -- ea756065] kind axis + json-merge landed; verified check PASS 9, foreign-key-preserving, fail-loud exit 3.**
**Who:** you (Python). **When:** first of the two; parallel-safe (mios-theme-render is Claude-owned but idle).
**What + How:**
1. **kind axis:** add optional `kind` to `[dotfiles.registry.<surface>]` ∈ `template|json-merge|ini-merge|registry|skip` (absent ⇒ `template`). `_load_surfaces` (~L46) returns a namedtuple `_Surface(name,template,target,section,kind,fixture,policy)` (so `_entry`/template call sites are untouched); it VALIDATES: unknown kind ⇒ `exit 3` (same posture as the missing-template `exit 3` at L64-67); a merge kind MUST declare `fixture.base` + `fixture.expected` else `exit 3` (no ungated merge surface — preserves the no-vacuous-green stance).
2. **dispatch:** module-level `_KINDS = {kind: (derive_fn, live_apply_fn)}`. `cmd_render`/`cmd_check` select `derive_fn` by `surface.kind` and keep the uniform shape `derive(surface,merged)->committed_target_bytes` then write/byte-diff (check NEVER reads a live foreign file — offline PR gate). `cmd_apply` selects `live_apply_fn`. template kind = today's `_render_text` whole-file (cmd_apply L343-393 UNCHANGED).
3. **json-merge derive/apply:** `owned = _render_text(tmpl, _resolved_for(merged,section))` is a MiOS-owned JSON SUBTREE (only the keys MiOS projects — e.g. VS Code `workbench.colorCustomizations` or WT `profiles.defaults`/`schemes[]`). `derive = json_merge(owned, fixture.base)`; `apply = json_merge(owned, LIVE dest) -> write-back + backup`. `json_merge`: (a) `_jsonc_to_json(base)` — a STRING-AWARE comment/trailing-comma strip that never mangles `https://` or a `//` inside a string value; if base won't parse ⇒ `exit 2`, write NOTHING (never corrupt an unreadable file). (b) `json.loads` (insertion-ordered). (c) deep-merge: recurse dicts; a MiOS-owned leaf/array wins (arrays REPLACED wholesale at the owned pointer — index-merge is unsafe); any key MiOS does NOT emit is byte-preserved. (d) `json.dump` with base indent, `ensure_ascii=False`.
4. **drift-gating for merge surfaces:** the surface declares `fixture.base` (a checked-in canonical base) + `fixture.expected` (the expected merged output); `check` = `derive(owned, fixture.base)` byte-diff `fixture.expected`. Add a proof `json-merge` surface (e.g. `vscode-colors`: a small MiOS-owned colorCustomizations template + a `fixture.base` that already contains a FOREIGN key + the `fixture.expected` with that foreign key preserved).
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/mios-theme-render` (`_load_surfaces`, `_KINDS`, `_jsonc_to_json`/`_json_deep_merge`, cmd_render/check/apply branch), `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (the new surface + `kind`/`fixture`), a new `.tmpl`, a fixtures dir (e.g. `usr/share/mios/theme/fixtures/`).
**Done When:** the 8 existing surfaces render/check **byte-identical** (kind defaults template — prove with the render byte-diff); the `json-merge` proof surface merges owned keys into `fixture.base` PRESERVING the foreign key (test); `check` gates it via the fixture; `apply` into a live temp target preserves foreign + writes a backup; unknown-kind / missing-fixture ⇒ `exit 3`; unparseable base ⇒ `exit 2` no-write; `capture` still template-only; `just drift-gate` green. Stage only your files.

## AGY-58  (WS-DOTFILES / ADR-0010, **P2**) — `ini-merge` mode + a `gitconfig-live` surface (UNBLOCK gitconfig live-apply)  **[DONE]**
> **[DONE by Claude on main -- 8f52ccfb] ini-merge + gitconfig-live landed; check PASS 10, live-apply preserves credential/signing/remotes + seeds owned + backup. gitconfig live-apply UNBLOCKED.**
**Who:** you (Python). **When:** after AGY-57 (needs the `kind` dispatch + `_KINDS`).
**What + How:**
1. **ini-merge derive/apply:** `_parse_ini(text)` keeps an ORDERED line-model + a `{(section,subsection,key)->line_idx}` index (preserves `#`/`;` comments + `[section "subsection"]` quoting). `owned_kv = _parse_ini(_render_text(gitconfig.tmpl))` — so MiOS owns EXACTLY the keys the template emits (`user.name/email`, `core.editor`, `init.defaultBranch`, `pull.rebase`, `alias.{co,br,ci,st}`). `_ini_upsert` per owned key: ABSENT in base ⇒ seed (append the section block if the section is absent); PRESENT ⇒ replace value in place per policy. Everything NOT in `owned_kv` (`credential`, `user.signingkey`, `commit.gpgsign`, remotes) is byte-preserved. Subsectioned keys keyed on `(section,subsection)` so they never collide; leave foreign multivar keys untouched (owned set is single-valued).
2. **merge write policy** `seed-or-enforce` (default for gitconfig, safety-first): per owned key — ABSENT ⇒ write (seed); PRESENT + `operator_set(key)` ⇒ write (enforce the operator's explicit SSOT choice); PRESENT + NOT `operator_set` ⇒ SKIP (never stomp an existing foreign value with a vendor default). `operator_set = mios_toml.load_merged(sec,key) != mios_toml.load_vendor(sec,key)` AND non-empty.
3. **gitconfig-live surface (NEW, ADDITIVE):** `kind=ini-merge`, `policy=seed-or-enforce`, `template` REUSES `usr/share/mios/theme/templates/gitconfig.tmpl` VERBATIM (the rendered template's `[section] key=value` lines ARE the ownership manifest AND the values), `apply.target.linux="~/.gitconfig"`, `apply.target.windows="%USERPROFILE%/.gitconfig"`, `fixture.base`/`fixture.expected` = a small canonical gitconfig pair. The EXISTING `gitconfig` surface (etc/skel/.gitconfig, kind=template, NO apply.target) stays BYTE-IDENTICAL. This is the safe unblock of the live-apply Claude withheld (whole-file overwrite would clobber the user credential/signing/remotes; ini-merge sets only the ~9 owned keys).
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/mios-theme-render` (`_parse_ini`/`_ini_upsert`/`_operator_set` + the ini-merge `_KINDS` entry), `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (the `gitconfig-live` surface), a fixtures pair.
**Done When:** the existing `gitconfig` (template) surface is byte-identical; `apply gitconfig-live` into a live `~/.gitconfig` seeded with a FOREIGN `[credential]`/`user.signingkey`/remote PRESERVES them (assert) while setting the owned keys; `seed-or-enforce` SKIPS an existing foreign `user.email` unless operator_set; `check` gates via the fixture; a backup is written on change; unknown/missing-fixture ⇒ `exit 3`; `just drift-gate` green. Stage only your files.

## AGY-59  (WS-DOTFILES / ADR-0010, **P2**) — the `registry` kind (Windows Registry apply) + a windows-registry proof surface  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python + PowerShell). **When:** after AGY-57/58 (landed).
**What + How:** add `registry` to `_KINDS`. Semantics (per the design): `derive` = build a manifest of the MiOS-owned Registry values (from `_render_text(tmpl,...)` -> a small JSON/INI-ish manifest of `HKCU\Path\Name = value` entries), byte-diffed against `fixture.expected` for the offline gate (check NEVER touches the live Registry). `live_apply_fn` = **Linux/non-Windows: no-op SKIP** (log "registry surface -- Windows-only, skipping"); **Windows (`os.name=='nt'`)**: apply each owned value via `reg add`/PowerShell `Set-ItemProperty` under HKCU only (refuse HKLM/system hives), reading back to confirm; back up the prior values first (export the owned keys to a `.mios-bak` .reg). Fail-loud: a registry surface missing `fixture.base/expected` => exit 3 (same as other merge kinds). Add ONE proof `windows-registry` surface (e.g. a couple of `HKCU\Console` or a MiOS app setting) with template + fixture pair; `apply.target` names the HKCU path root.
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/mios-theme-render` (`_KINDS` registry entry + `_registry_derive`/`_registry_apply`), `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (the surface), a template + fixtures pair.
**Done When:** the 10 existing surfaces stay byte-identical (`check` PASS 11); on Linux `apply <registry-surface>` is a clean no-op skip; the offline `check` gates it via the fixture; missing-fixture/unknown => exit 3; `py_compile` clean. Stage only your files.

### AGY-84 REFINEMENT (Claude, VERIFIED against HEAD 3551d6d5) -- do NOT naive-emit ~500 vars
The drift-check "referenced but NOT emitted (a consumer would lose its var)" is OVER-BROAD. I read two representatives and confirmed the premise is false for the bulk:
- `server.py:804` `MIOS_ACI_HEAD_FRAC` = `_cfg_num(_ACI_TOML,"MIOS_ACI_HEAD_FRAC","head_frac",0.6,float)` -> reads `[aci].head_frac` from mios.toml SSOT with default; env is an OPTIONAL override. Consumer canNOT lose its var.
- `test_mios_template.py:48` `MIOS_NONEXISTENT_ENV` = deliberate test sentinel ("default absent -> fallback"). Must NEVER be emitted.
**Fix the CHECK to its true intent (flag only a var a consumer would GENUINELY lose), by category:**
- **A. SSOT-section reads (bulk):** `_cfg_num`/`_cfg_bool`/`_cfg_str`/`_toml_section(...)` with an env-name arg AND a default -> COMPLIANT (var IS SSOT-sourced). Recognize these; do not flag. This is most of server.py / mios_pipe/**/config.py / kernel.
- **B. test/negative sentinels:** anything referenced ONLY under `test_*.py`, `tests/`, `*-negatives.sh`, or the `38-ssot-lint.sh` fixtures (MIOS_NONEXISTENT_ENV, MIOS_NO_SUCH_VAR_XYZ, MIOS_FOO, MIOS_FIXTURE_*, MIOS_AI_TEST_TEMP, MIOS_BENCH, MIOS_EGRESS_TOOL, MIOS_MTLS_TOOL, MIOS_AUTONOMOUS_PRIORITY, MIOS_ANSWER_CHUNK_CHARS...) -> EXCLUDE from the scan.
- **C. regex prefix fragments:** trailing `_` or `__` captures (MIOS_A2O_, MIOS_AGENT_, MIOS_PORT_, MIOS_NATIVE_LOOP_, MIOS_DRIVE__, MIOS_COLS__, MIOS_CELL_H__ ...) -> tighten the extraction regex so it does NOT capture partial identifiers / `${MIOS_x_}${suffix}` interpolation stems.
- **D. Windows PowerShell vars:** any hit in `*.ps1` (Get-MiOS.ps1, build-mios.ps1, mios-pipeline.ps1) is a WinPS var -> NOT a Linux env key; userenv.sh (bash) neither can nor should emit it. Route to the PS globals.ps1 SSOT lane; EXCLUDE `.ps1` from the "must be emitted by userenv.sh" scan.
- **E. genuine residue:** after A-D, the SMALL set of real bash/systemd consumers that read `${MIOS_X}` with NO default and NO SSOT-section read -> THESE emit from the auto-derived key library (WS-NAME), sourced from mios.toml, reconciling MIOS_AI_VLLM_* <-> short MIOS_VLLM_*.
**Done (unchanged):** 0 GENUINE referenced-but-not-emitted; A-D no longer flagged; twins identical (drift-27); add a negative test PER category (a Cat-A `_cfg_num` var, a Cat-B sentinel, a Cat-D `.ps1` var) proving they're correctly NOT flagged, and one Cat-E var proving a real orphan IS flagged until emitted.

## AGY-89  (WS-RELTOP hardening, **P1**) -- globals.sh clobbers the SSOT image name (audit-confirmed)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (your WS-RELTOP/T-252 code). An independent audit found your credential-driven selection has a real latent bug + a misleading framing:
- **Bug (E1):** `automation/lib/globals.sh:109-113` -- the no-creds branch does `export MIOS_IMAGE_NAME="localhost/mios"` UNCONDITIONALLY. `common.sh` sources `userenv.sh` FIRST (ghcr from SSOT) then `globals.sh`, so on a no-creds host this OVERWRITES userenv's ghcr with localhost -- contradicting your own `common.sh:48-52` comment ("globals fills gaps") and the "userenv stays faithful to SSOT" claim. It also leaves `MIOS_IMAGE_NAME=localhost/mios` while `MIOS_IMAGE_REF` stays ghcr (`:=` at :116) -> an inconsistent name/ref pair. Harmless TODAY only because no reachable consumer pulls via `$MIOS_IMAGE_NAME` (consumers are display/legacy/CI-sources-userenv-directly) -- but a landmine: any future `bootc switch`/`podman pull "$MIOS_IMAGE_NAME"` after `common.sh` on a no-cred host -> `localhost/mios` -> crash.
- **Framing:** the real build path (`usr/libexec/mios/mios-build-driver:766`) HARDCODES `localhost/mios:latest` and never sources globals; the fresh Windows/Linux entry points never source globals either -> the credential-selection block is effectively dead on all fresh paths.
**Fix:** replace the whole `_has_registry_creds` if/else (`globals.sh:~101-113`) with a non-clobbering default: `: "${MIOS_IMAGE_NAME:=ghcr.io/mios-dev/mios}"` (MIOS_LOCAL_IMAGE already carries the localhost tag; keep credential gating only on a build-vs-publish DECISION, never on the image NAME).
- **E4 (twin parity):** `automation/lib/globals.ps1:62-92` respects `$env:MIOS_IMAGE_NAME` in BOTH branches while `globals.sh` forces localhost in the no-creds branch -> the two "SSOT" twins disagree on override semantics. Align them (both = non-clobbering `:=` default to ghcr, local tag via MIOS_LOCAL_IMAGE).
**Done:** no-creds host keeps `MIOS_IMAGE_NAME=ghcr.io/mios-dev/mios` (name/ref consistent); sh+ps1 twins equivalent; drift-gate (41/45 resolver-twin) stays green; add a negative test asserting a no-creds `common.sh` load does NOT downgrade MIOS_IMAGE_NAME to localhost.

## AGY-92  (audit BLOCKERS, **P0** -- deploy/user-facing breakage that ships GREEN gates) -- fix FIRST  **[DONE]**
An adversarial audit vetted 8 of your workstreams (ALL needs-refinement). Claude spot-verified B2+B4 directly. Each ships a GREEN drift-gate because a fixture/generator encodes the wrong behavior -> the gate cannot catch it.

**B1 (WS-BLADE -- deploy-time bootc failure):** the MIOSSYS refactor (c47ed570) moved bakes to `57-mios-sys-build.sh` + `mios-bake-group extra`, but `automation/08-system-files-overlay.sh:173-184` still `ln -sf`'s EVERY `.image` quadlet into `/usr/lib/bootc/bound-images.d/`. `mios-llm-heavy-alt.image` (sglang, group sys) + `mios-llama-swap.image` (llama-swap, group cuda) are now in NO baked group -> `57` builds localhost/mios-sys + localhost/mios-cuda via `COPY --from` but never pulls those engines into $STORE as resolvable bound refs -> `bootc` "does not resolve to an image ID" at INSTALL. **Fix:** re-add a `mios-bake-group sys` + `mios-bake-group cuda` RUN (per the [build.bake] "one RUN per group" comment) OR retire the two bound `.image` quadlets so 08-overlay stops binding them. Add a build-time assertion that every bound-images.d symlink resolves.

**B2 (WS-CONFIG -- Save DOA; VERIFIED by Claude):** `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/kernel/config.py:537-549` range-checks every `[ports].*` as 1..65535, but `mios.toml:7775 [ports].stack_id = 0` is a port-OFFSET MULTIPLIER, not a port. `validate_config(full-toml)` -> ok=False "[ports].stack_id = 0 is out of the valid 1-65535 range"; omitting [ports] trips the "Refusing to drop critical [ports]" guard (:527). The configurator POSTs full TOML -> **422 for every operator, zero edits**. **Fix:** exempt non-listening keys (denylist `stack_id`, or only range-check keys that resolve to a real listening port). Add a round-trip test over the committed mios.toml.

**B3 (ADR-0010 -- breaks ALL ssh):** the dotfiles surface reuses `kind="ini-merge"` (git-config INI parser) for OpenSSH `ssh-config` -> emits `[host "mios-*"]` bracketed headers with `=`, which OpenSSH rejects (`Bad configuration option`). `mios.toml` maps apply.target -> `~/.ssh/config`, so `mios-dotfiles-render apply ssh-config` writes an unparseable file. Gate is green only because `fixtures/ssh-config.expected` encodes the SAME wrong format. **Fix:** add a dedicated ssh-config kind (or plain-template surface) emitting real stanza syntax (`Host mios-*` + tab-indented keywords, NO brackets/`=`); regenerate `.base`/`.expected`.

**B4 (liquid-glass -- work is DEAD; VERIFIED by Claude):** `automation/54-bake-hyprland.sh:13` does `cat << 'EOF' > /usr/share/mios/hyprland/hyprland.conf` -- the build UNCONDITIONALLY regenerates the file from the inline heredoc (still rounding=10/passes=3, no vibrancy/liquid beziers), DISCARDING every edit to the tracked artifact. **Fix:** apply the liquid-glass + spring-curve changes to the heredoc INSIDE 54-bake-hyprland.sh (the real source), preserving the `@@MIOS_COLOR_*@@` placeholders + Cockpit windowrules/`$mainMod, C` keybind; then delete/gitignore the tracked artifact OR add a drift-check asserting artifact == generator output.

**H1 (CI publish -- empty image name):** `.github/workflows/mios-ci.yml:308` sources userenv but never sets MIOS_VENDOR_TOML -> on stock ubuntu runners MIOS_IMAGE_NAME is UNSET -> malformed `:latest` tags + `podman login ''`; PUBLISH=true (:38) so NOT gated on GitHub. **Fix:** `export MIOS_VENDOR_TOML="$GITHUB_WORKSPACE/mios.toml"` before sourcing, or `: "${MIOS_IMAGE_NAME:=${REGISTRY}/${IMAGE_NAME}}"`.

**H2 (globals clobber -- AGY-89 STILL unfixed + WIDENED):** `globals.sh:112` still `export MIOS_IMAGE_NAME="localhost/mios"` unconditionally; worse, `0bd49e4b` now emits it into `system-sync-env.sh:234` -> PERSISTS the clobber into the runtime env file. **Fix (= AGY-89):** `: "${MIOS_IMAGE_NAME:=ghcr.io/mios-dev/mios}"`.

**Done:** all 4 blockers fixed; the masking fixtures/generators corrected so the green gate becomes meaningful; build-time/round-trip assertions added; drift-gate green FOR THE RIGHT REASON. Full 29-item follow-up batch + refinements arrive via Claude's finalization master-plan.

## AGY-95  (DB-driven accounts / "PostgresOS" wiring + peer-OS cleanup -- from the 2026-07-18 research)  **[DONE]**
Translates the PostgresOS + peer-OS research (full docs: `C:\MiOS\docs\agy\doc-postgresos-accounts.md`, `doc-peer-os-landscape.md`) into concrete work. - **P2 -- Linux DB-accounts projection (adopt_now):** project each `[accounts]` row -> a generated `/usr/lib/userdb/<name>.user` JSON drop-in (systemd userdb; nss-systemd synthesizes passwd/shadow/group). This is the PRIMARY Linux mechanism and matches MiOS's SSOT-projection + drift-gate model EXACTLY (same shape as mios-theme-render / the dotfiles registry). libnss-pgsql is archived/dead -- mine its schema, do NOT ship it.
- **P2 -- lldap-over-Postgres (adopt_now):** evaluate as the cross-platform SSOT LDAP face (users/groups stay in Postgres, fronted as RFC2307 LDAP; consumed by SSSD/nslcd) so Postgres stays the accounts SSOT without a second directory store. Alt: OpenLDAP back-sql (experimental), Keycloak+SCIM (owns its own schema -> route writes through its API).
- **P2 -- ADR:** record that "PostgresOS" is a MiOS CONCEPT NAME mapped to a concrete stack (systemd userdb projection + lldap + SCIM-as-pattern + the autounattend/SetupComplete `New-LocalUser` Windows path), and that there is NO upstream repo by that name -- so contributors don't chase a nonexistent project.
- **P3 -- peer-OS cleanup:** "PodmanOS" is NOT a real product either; the reality is `quay.io/podman/machine-os` (FCOS + bootc-switch). Close out any ROADMAP/doc reference to a distinct "PodmanOS". Positioning (for docs): Bluefin/Bluespeed = closest competitor on the same substrate; Hummingbird = watch (distroless-vs-virt-floor); bootc-image-builder repo is archived -> point at osbuild/image-builder.
**Windows side is Claude's (mios-bootstrap, DONE):** the RID-500 rename moved OUT of the specialize pass INTO SetupComplete.cmd (build/first-boot, DB-fed baseline -- Windows has NO NSS/PAM); the `MiOS-AccountSync` MINUTE task (gated on db_backed) handles ongoing DB->Windows-account reconcile.
**Done:** db_backed no longer inert (+ drift-gated); Linux userdb projection landed + drift-gated; lldap decision recorded; ADR written; PodmanOS reference closed.

## AGY-96  (WS-HARDEN / build-net, **P1**) — curl-retry drift-gate [DONE] ✔  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash + drift-gate). **When:** FIRST — highest leverage; a ~20h publish loss traced to ONE retry-less `curl`.
**What + How:** the build died repeatedly on a transient GitHub `504` because `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile`'s AdGuard `RUN curl … | tar` had no `--retry`. Claude hand-swept every fetch; now ENFORCE it. Add a check to `automation/38-drift-checks.sh` (next free number) that greps every build-time network fetch and FAILS on any lacking a retry flag:
- `RUN … curl …` / bare `curl … http(s)://` across all `**/Containerfile` (sys, agents, cuda), `automation/*.sh`, `automation/lib/*.sh`, `usr/libexec/mios/*.sh`, `automation/90-generate-sbom.sh`.
- COMPLIANT iff it carries `--retry` OR routes through `scurl` (which injects retry) OR is explicitly listed in a `mios.toml [build.net].retry_exempt` allowlist (document each exemption).
- Recognize `wget` (`--tries`) too. Read-only, fast, no built image.
**Where:** `automation/38-drift-checks.sh`, `usr/share/mios/mios.toml` (`[build.net].retry_exempt` if needed).
**Done When:** the gate flags an injected retry-less `curl`, passes clean on HEAD (all fetches now retried), + a negative test proving the injected case is caught; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-97  (WS-HARDEN / scurl correctness, **P1**) — fix the scurl binary-stream corruption + harden its arg-parser  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash). **When:** next — this is a real DATA-CORRUPTION bug, not just hygiene.
**What + How:** `automation/lib/masking.sh` `scurl()` sets `is_binary` ONLY when it sees `-o/-O/--output`. But the build's dominant pattern is `curl … | tar` (stream to stdout, NO `-o`) — scurl classifies that as **text** and pipes the tarball through `mask_filter` (`sed -u`), **corrupting the binary stream**. (1) Fix classification: NEVER run `mask_filter` on a body destined for a pipe/download — only mask human-facing text (e.g. gate masking on stdout being a TTY, or drop mask_filter for any non-`-o` streamed body). (2) Harden the naive parser: it takes the FIRST `https?://` arg as the URL and misses `--output=FILE`, `--url X`, and mis-detects a `-H` header value that looks like a URL. (3) Add `automation/lib/test_masking.sh`: asserts a piped binary body is byte-identical (no sed mangling), a github URL gets the auth header AND the token is masked in TEXT output, and `--output=` is detected.
**Where:** `automation/lib/masking.sh`, new `automation/lib/test_masking.sh`, wire into the shell-test/lint gate.
**Done When:** `curl … | tar` through scurl is byte-identical to raw curl; parser handles the 3 edge cases; test green; `just drift-gate` green.

**Where:** new `usr/lib/mios/mios_env.py`, `usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/__init__.py`, new `test_mios_env.py`, (optionally) `usr/lib/mios/userenv.sh`.
**Done When:** one strip helper (no duplicate); the empty-env import test is green across all agent-pipe modules; `just drift-gate` green.

**Who:** you (SQL + bash/awk drift-gate). **What+How:** schema-init.sql is inconsistent: the 4 original vector indexes (knowledge:42, agent_memory:72, mios_rag:452, config_kv:567) carry the canonical `WITH (m = 16, ef_construction = 64)`, but the ~19 newer HNSW indexes added by AGY-7/11/18/22 (skill/verb/tool_call/directory_entry:635-638, package_set..preset:654-776, event/session:783-788, account_preference:819) OMIT the params and silently ride pgvector defaults, contradicting the AGY-3/7/11 'mirror the exact DDL' rule; `mios_identity.account_preferences.emb` (1139) has NO index at all and a mismatched `emb_version varchar(32)` vs the varchar(64) used everywhere else. Add the explicit `WITH (m=16, ef_construction=64)` to every param-less HNSW index (idempotent — CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS already guards, so it is a no-op on existing DBs but pins intent), and unify the outlier emb_version width. Add a new `check_vector_schema_hygiene` to automation/38-drift-checks.sh (next free number, 63+) that greps every `USING hnsw` line and fails if any lacks the canonical params, and asserts every `emb vector(768)` column has sibling `emb_model`+`emb_version`.
**Where:** usr/share/mios/postgres/schema-init.sql, automation/38-drift-checks.sh. **Done When:** `psql -f schema-init.sql` re-applies idempotently (no-op on an existing DB); the new check is GREEN on HEAD and RED on a fixture where an HNSW index is stripped of its params or an emb column loses provenance; `just drift-gate` passes.

**Where:** usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/memory/embed_backfill.py, usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/test_mios_embed_backfill.py, automation/38-drift-checks.sh. **Done When:** backfill populates config_kv.emb/account_preference.emb over their text projection with provenance (verified with a monkeypatched pg + embed client in test_mios_embed_backfill.py); a 2nd run is a near-noop; the coverage gate is GREEN on HEAD and RED when an emb column is added with no PK_MAP/exempt entry; `just drift-gate` passes.

## AGY-184  (WS-VECTOR, **P2**) — AGY-125 — reconcile the duplicate account-preference tables + fix allocate_gid() UPG collision  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (SQL + Python). **What+How:** Two competing preference tables exist: `account_preference` (public, AGY-22 line 809 — layer-based, HNSW-indexed, emb_model+emb_version, and the ONLY one read, by materialize-user-config.py:87) versus the dead `mios_identity.account_preferences` (T-246, line 1134 — UUID/pref_key, NO reader or writer anywhere, NO HNSW index, NO emb_model, mismatched emb_version varchar(32)). Also fix `allocate_gid()` (lines 803-807) which draws from the SAME `uid_alloc` sequence as `allocate_uid()`, making uid==gid user-private-group impossible (a fresh user gets uid=1000, gid=1001); give gid its own sequence OR make allocate_gid mirror the uid for UPG. **Where:** usr/share/mios/postgres/schema-init.sql, usr/libexec/mios/materialize-user-config.py, usr/libexec/mios/seed-db-config.py, automation/38-drift-checks.sh, usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/test_mios_account_sync.py. **Done When:** schema applies idempotently; only one preference surface remains (verified by the new gate, which goes RED if a second `*account_preference*` table reappears); allocate_uid/allocate_gid yield a UPG-consistent pair asserted in a test; materialize-user-config still round-trips a seeded pref; `just drift-gate` passes.

## AGY-242  (WS-OTEL, **P1**) — W3C traceparent inbound-adopt + outbound-propagate interop  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python). **What+How:** observability/trace.py new_trace_id() mints a 16-hex (64-bit) id and server.py (~335-345) propagates a custom `X-MiOS-Trace` header only, so MiOS traces never stitch to a standard W3C-compliant collector or peer. Add W3C Trace Context: widen new_trace_id() to 32-hex (128-bit) keeping new_span_id() at 16-hex, add pure helpers `format_traceparent(trace_id, span_id, sampled)` / `parse_traceparent(header)` (validate `00-<32hex>-<16hex>-<2hex>`), adopt an inbound `traceparent` in server.py's request-mint path, and emit it outbound on the A2A (a2a_client._a2a_send_message_to_peer), MCP (mcp._mcp_http_rpc) and Hermes hops alongside the legacy header.
**Where:** usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/observability/trace.py, usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/server.py, usr/lib/mios/agent-pipe/mios_pipe/federation/a2a_client.py, mios_pipe/federation/mcp.py, test_mios_trace.py. **Done When:** test_mios_trace.py asserts parse(format(id,span,True)) round-trips, rejects a malformed traceparent, and confirms new_trace_id() is 32-hex; a test drives an outbound call stub and asserts a valid `traceparent` header is set; existing trace tests still pass; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-244  (WS-FED, **P1**) — Enforce AgentCard signature verification on peer probe  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python). **What+How:** a2a_client._a2a_fetch_card / _a2a_probe_peer fetch a peer's AgentCard and index its skills WITHOUT ever calling a2a.py's `_verify_agent_card_signature`, so a signed card's issuer is never checked and a forged card is trusted blindly. **Who:** you (Python). **What+How:** mcp._mcp_probe_server (line ~490) calls `tools/list` exactly once (rid=2), reads `result.tools`, and IGNORES `result.nextCursor` — per the MCP spec a server with more tools than one page returns a cursor, so MiOS silently registers only page 1 and drops the rest. ## AGY-260  (WS-FLOAT, **P1**) — Extract one shared build-time latest-tag resolver (releases/latest redirect, no GitHub API)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash). **What+How:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` inlines the SAME `t="$(curl -fsSL -o /dev/null -w '%{url_effective}' --retry 5 --connect-timeout 20 <repo>/releases/latest | sed 's#.*/tag/##')"` one-liner eight times (matchbox L46, forgejo L49, forgejo-runner L52, jaeger L55, crowdsec L59, k3s L62, code-server L65, firecrawl L10 & L111) — and no shared helper exists (grep for `url_effective` across `automation/lib` returns nothing). Author ONE resolver used by both contexts: (1) a bash function `mios_resolve_latest_tag <releases-base-url>` added to `automation/lib/common.sh` (next to `record_version`, line 103) implementing the redirect-follow (`curl -fsSL -o /dev/null -w '%{url_effective}' --retry 5 --retry-all-errors --connect-timeout 20 "$1/releases/latest" | sed 's#.*/tag/##'`), echoing the bare tag, non-fatal (returns empty on failure so callers degrade-open); (2) a standalone, dependency-free `usr/libexec/mios/mios-resolve-latest` (same logic, `#!/bin/bash`, `set -uo pipefail`) so the sys Containerfile can `COPY` + `RUN` it inside the nested build where `lib/common.sh` is not sourced. Use the redirect (NOT `api.github.com`) so there is NO rate limit. Add a `usr/libexec/mios/test_mios_resolve_latest.sh` unit that stubs `curl` via a PATH shim and asserts a `/tag/v1.2.3`-style effective URL yields `v1.2.3` and a curl failure yields empty + exit 0. **Where:** `automation/lib/common.sh`, `usr/libexec/mios/mios-resolve-latest` (new), `usr/libexec/mios/test_mios_resolve_latest.sh` (new). **Done When:** `bash -n automation/lib/common.sh usr/libexec/mios/mios-resolve-latest` clean; the unit test passes (stubbed-curl tag extraction + degrade-open empty case); `just drift-gate` green.

**Who:** you (Python + drift-gate). **What+How:** The `[build.float].allow` allowlist (AGY-263) is only trustworthy if each exception is justified and none rot. Add `check_float_allowlist_documented` to `automation/38-drift-checks.sh` (label (73)): load `[build.float].allow` via tomllib and assert (a) every entry has a non-empty `key`, `ref`, `reason`, and `upstream` (a well-formed URL or `https?://`), and (b) every entry's `key` is actually REFERENCED somewhere in the tree (grep the `ref`/component across `automation`, `usr/libexec`, `usr/share/mios/**/Containerfile*`, `mios.toml`) — a dead allowlist entry (nothing pins it anymore) reds so stale exceptions are pruned. Add negative tests to `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh`: (1) an entry with an empty `reason` reds; (2) an entry whose `key` matches nothing reds; both restore green. **Where:** `automation/38-drift-checks.sh`, `usr/share/mios/mios.toml [build.float]`, `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh`. **Done When:** the check is green at HEAD with the seeded exceptions; a reason-less entry and a dead entry each red; negatives restore; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-272  (WS-FLOAT, **P2**) — Grep-gate: ban the rate-limited api.github.com release-lookup mechanism  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash + Python + drift-gate). **What+How:** After AGY-262 converts the API callers to the redirect resolver, add `check_no_github_api_release` to `automation/38-drift-checks.sh` (label (74)) that greps `automation/**/*.sh`, `usr/libexec/mios/**`, and `usr/share/mios/**/Containerfile*` for `api.github.com/repos/[^ ]*/releases` (the rate-limited REST endpoint) and reds on any hit — forcing all future tag resolution through the redirect-follow (`mios-resolve-latest` / `mios_resolve_latest_tag`, no rate limit, per the float directive "no GitHub API"). Allowlist nothing except test fixtures + reference docs (mirror the exclusion style of `check_curl_retry`, which skips `test-drift-gates.sh`). Add a negative test in `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh` injecting a `curl https://api.github.com/repos/x/y/releases/latest` line and asserting red, then restore. **Where:** `automation/38-drift-checks.sh`, `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh`. **Done When:** the gate is green at HEAD (post-AGY-262); the negative test reds on an injected api.github.com release call then restores; the resolver-based redirect calls do not trip it; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-274  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — Bake-time per-stack smoke RUN in sys/Containerfile (import+exec assert)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Containerfile / bash). **When:** first — it is the foundation the other WS-BAKE tasks harden.
**What + How:** the mios-sys stacks currently install into `/opt/*-venv` but nothing proves they can actually START before the image ships — SearXNG's `msgspec`/`setuptools.build_meta` class of failure installs "successfully" then dies at import. Add a **smoke-verification `RUN`** at the END of `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (after the firecrawl COPY at L119; there is NO `bootc container lint` final-line requirement on the sys Containerfile — check 38 only scans root `Containerfile`/`Containerfile.minimal`, so appending a RUN is legal). The RUN must `set -e` and assert each stack imports/execs, failing the whole build on the first broken stack:
- SearXNG: `/opt/searxng-venv/bin/python -c "import searx, msgspec"` (msgspec explicit — that was the real runtime miss).
- Open WebUI: `/opt/open-webui-venv/bin/python -c "import open_webui"` **and** `/opt/open-webui-venv/bin/open-webui --help >/dev/null`.
- Crawl4AI: `/opt/crawl4ai-venv/bin/python -c "import crawl4ai, camoufox, playwright, fastapi, uvicorn, pydantic"`.
- Firecrawl: assert the built output exists (`test -d /opt/firecrawl/apps/api/dist`) and `node -e "process.exit(0)"` runs under the image's node.
Each assertion echoes a `[sys-smoke] <stack> OK` line so a bake log shows exactly which stack passed. Do NOT add retries here — a smoke FAILURE must be fatal (that is the point).
**Where:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (append one `RUN` block after L119).
**Done When:** the Containerfile still parses (a `podman build --help`-level `hadolint`/`bash -n` on the extracted RUN body is clean — verify the RUN's shell with `bash -n` on the extracted script); the smoke block names all four stacks; `just drift-gate` stays green (no new violation); stage ONLY `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile`.

## AGY-275  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — Extract the smoke assertions into a COPY'd `smoke.sh` + a host-side parity test  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash + tests). **When:** after AGY-274.
**What + How:** inlining the AGY-274 assertions in a `RUN` makes them unmaintainable and impossible to unit-check. Factor them into `usr/share/mios/sys/smoke.sh` (a real `#!/usr/bin/env bash`, `set -euo pipefail` script that runs every stack assertion and exits non-zero on the first failure), `COPY` it into the sys image and invoke it from the AGY-274 RUN (`COPY smoke.sh /tmp/smoke.sh` then `RUN bash /tmp/smoke.sh`). Then add a **host-side static test** `tests/sys-smoke-parity.sh` that parses `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` for every `python3 -m venv /opt/<name>-venv` (and the two symlink/venv stanzas) and asserts `smoke.sh` references each `/opt/<name>-venv` — so a future stack added to the Containerfile without a smoke assertion FAILS the test. Wire `tests/sys-smoke-parity.sh` into the `drift-gate` recipe in `Justfile` next to the existing `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh` invocation.
**Where:** new `usr/share/mios/sys/smoke.sh`, `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (COPY+RUN), new `tests/sys-smoke-parity.sh`, `Justfile` (drift-gate recipe).
**Done When:** `bash -n usr/share/mios/sys/smoke.sh` and `bash -n tests/sys-smoke-parity.sh` are clean; the parity test PASSES on the current tree and FAILS when a `*-venv` line is added to the Containerfile with no matching `smoke.sh` reference (demonstrate with a scratch copy); `just drift-gate` green; stage only those four explicit paths.

## AGY-276  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — Newest-supported-interpreter resolver helper (de-hardcode `python3.12`)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash). **When:** independent of 274/275.
**What + How:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` L92 hardcodes `python3.12` for the Open WebUI venv because open-webui caps `requires_python <3.13` while Fedora 44's default `python3` is 3.14. That "newest interpreter the floated package supports" is a **compatibility constraint the operator should never hand-maintain** (ADR-0003 float-latest). Write `usr/share/mios/sys/pick-python.sh <pkg>` that: (1) enumerates the `python3.N` interpreters present on PATH (`compgen -c 'python3.'` / glob `/usr/bin/python3.[0-9]*`), newest-first; (2) resolves the floated package's `requires_python` from the PyPI JSON API (`https://pypi.org/pypi/<pkg>/json`, `.info.requires_python`) with `curl --retry 5` (check 64 requires the `--retry`) — degrade-open to the newest interpreter if the API is unreachable; (3) prints the newest `python3.N` that satisfies the specifier via a small pure-python `packaging.specifiers`/`SpecifierSet` check run under a candidate interpreter, or the base `python3` if none needed. Then replace L92's literal `python3.12` with `"$(bash /tmp/pick-python.sh open-webui)"` (COPY the helper in). Keep the existing L88-91 comment updated to point at the helper.
**Where:** new `usr/share/mios/sys/pick-python.sh`, `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (COPY + L92 substitution + comment).
**Done When:** `bash -n usr/share/mios/sys/pick-python.sh` clean; run it locally against a mocked candidate set (`PATH`-shim `python3.12`/`python3.14` stubs) and confirm it selects the newest satisfying interpreter and degrades-open when the JSON fetch fails; no bare `python3.12` literal remains in the Containerfile except inside a comment; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the two paths.

## AGY-279  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — camoufox + playwright browser fetch: retry-with-backoff + degrade-open  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Containerfile / bash). **When:** independent.
**What + How:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` L105 (`/opt/crawl4ai-venv/bin/python3 -m camoufox fetch`) and the implicit `playwright` browser download are single-shot network pulls with NO retry — a transient CDN blip aborts the whole bake. Split the browser fetch out of the pip install `RUN` into its own `RUN` that: retries `camoufox fetch` (and `playwright install chromium` if crawl4ai needs it — verify against crawl4ai's runtime) up to 3× with `10s/20s` backoff (mirror the `build_image_retry` backoff in `usr/libexec/mios/mios-webtools-firstboot.sh` L44-56); and **degrades open** — if the browser download still fails after retries, log `[sys-bake] camoufox/playwright browser deferred to firstboot` and `|| true` so the bake proceeds (the browser is a firstboot-fetchable asset, consistent with Law 12 BAKE-NOT-FETCH's degrade-open posture that check 39 enforces). The pip install of the packages themselves stays FATAL (only the browser binary download degrades).
**Where:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (split L97-105 crawl4ai stanza).
**Done When:** the extracted retry loop passes `bash -n`; the browser-download step degrades open (`|| true` after the retry loop) while the package `pip install` remains fatal; `just drift-gate` green (curl/network lines, if any added, carry `--retry` per check 64); stage only the Containerfile.

## AGY-280  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — Firecrawl v2 shared-object runtime-path verification RUN  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Containerfile / bash). **When:** independent.
**What + How:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` L117-119 COPYs the Go `html-to-markdown.so` into `apps/api/sharedLibs/go-html-to-md/` because Firecrawl **v2 relocated** it (was `apps/api/src/lib/go-html-to-md/`). A future v2→v3 restructure would silently move the load path again and the COPY would land the `.so` where nothing reads it — no bake error, a broken runtime. Add a verification `RUN` immediately after L119 that: (1) asserts the `.so` exists at the copied path AND is a valid shared object (`file /opt/firecrawl/apps/api/sharedLibs/go-html-to-md/html-to-markdown.so | grep -q 'shared object'`); (2) greps the BUILT firecrawl output (`/opt/firecrawl/apps/api/dist`) for the string `sharedLibs/go-html-to-md` (or whatever path token the runtime `koffi`/`ffi` loader references) and FAILS with a clear `[sys-bake] firecrawl .so load-path drifted -- upstream relocated go-html-to-md` message if the built code no longer references the directory the `.so` was placed in. This turns a silent runtime break into a loud bake failure.
**Where:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (new `RUN` after L119).
**Done When:** the extracted RUN body passes `bash -n`; the check asserts BOTH `.so` validity and that the built code references the placement dir; the failure message names the upstream-relocation cause; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the Containerfile.

## AGY-282  (WS-BAKE, **P1**) — Nested-build mount + scratch cleanup trap (mount-leak hardening)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash / podman). **When:** independent.
**What + How:** `usr/libexec/mios/57-mios-sys-build.sh` and `usr/libexec/mios/mios-bake-group` both run a **nested** `podman --root "$STORE" --runroot "$SCRATCH/run" build/pull` inside the outer OCI build. On a failure mid-build (both are `set -euo pipefail`) they exit WITHOUT tearing down the inner store's overlay mounts or purging `$SCRATCH` — on a retried CI runner this leaks overlay/loopback mounts and fills the runner disk (a documented WS-BAKE failure mode). Add an `EXIT` trap to BOTH scripts that: runs `podman --root "$STORE" --runroot "$SCRATCH/run" unshare umount ...`/`podman ... system prune` best-effort, force-unmounts any lingering mount under `$SCRATCH` (`awk '$2 ~ SCRATCH {print $2}' /proc/self/mountinfo | xargs -r umount -l`), and `rm -rf "${SCRATCH:?}"` — all guarded `|| true` so cleanup never masks the real exit code (`trap '_cleanup' EXIT`; preserve `$?`). In `mios-bake-group` respect the existing `MIOS_BAKE_BOUND_IMAGES=0` early-exit (L47-50) so the trap is a no-op there. Reference the existing partial cleanup at `mios-bake-group` L58/L140.
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/57-mios-sys-build.sh`, `usr/libexec/mios/mios-bake-group`.
**Done When:** `bash -n` clean on both; the trap fires on both success and forced-failure paths (test with an injected `false`) and never alters the propagated exit code; `${SCRATCH:?}` guard present so an unset var can't `rm -rf /`; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the two scripts.

## AGY-283  (WS-BAKE, **P2**) — Idempotent SBOM `bound-images.tsv` append (retry-safe)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash). **When:** independent.
**What + How:** `usr/libexec/mios/57-mios-sys-build.sh` L99-100 and `usr/libexec/mios/mios-bake-group` L135 append (`>>`) rows to `$SBOM_DIR/bound-images.tsv` with no dedup — a retried build (the whole point of WS-BAKE robustness) writes DUPLICATE rows for the same image, and `tools/generate-bake-plan.py`'s SBOM reader (L181-190) keeps the LAST digest so duplicates are silent bloat/ambiguity. Make the append idempotent: before writing a row, drop any existing line for that `image` key (`grep -v -P "^<img>\t" the file to a tmp, then append the fresh row`, or an awk last-wins rewrite). Keep the TSV header handling intact. Do this in both writers via a tiny shared `_sbom_put <img> <digest> <group>` shell function (define it inline in each script — they don't share a lib today; do NOT invent a new sourced lib for two call sites).
**Where:** `usr/libexec/mios/57-mios-sys-build.sh`, `usr/libexec/mios/mios-bake-group`.
**Done When:** `bash -n` clean on both; running either writer twice for the same image yields exactly ONE row (verify with a scratch `$SBOM_DIR`); the header line survives; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the two scripts.

Add a negative fixture to `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh` that swaps two lines and asserts failure.
**Where:** `automation/38-drift-checks.sh`, `tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh`.
**Done When:** `bash -n` clean on both; the gate PASSES on the current Containerfile and FAILS when the setuptools/`-e` lines are reordered; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the two files.

## AGY-285  (WS-BAKE, **P2**) — Retry-wrap the `git clone`s in sys/Containerfile (clone-flake hardening)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Containerfile / bash). **When:** independent.
**What + How:** the existing `check_curl_retry` (check 64) enforces `--retry` on curl/wget only — it does NOT cover `git clone`, and the three sys clones (`usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` L11 firecrawl go-builder, L82 searxng, L112 firecrawl) have NO retry, so a transient GitHub/Codeberg blip aborts the bake. Wrap each `git clone --depth 1 --branch "$t" <url> <dst>` in a 3×-with-backoff retry loop (a shell `for a in 1 2 3; do git clone ... && break || sleep $((a*5)); done; test -d <dst>/.git` idiom, or a small `_git_clone_retry` function defined at the top of each `RUN`). Preserve the exact `--branch "$t"`/`--depth 1` args so `check_containerfile_pinned_clones` (check 71) stays green (it requires `--branch`/`--tag`/ref). Keep the resolved-tag `curl --retry 5` redirect lines (L10, L46-66, L111) untouched — they already retry.
**Where:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (L11, L82, L112 clone stanzas).
**Done When:** extracted retry loops pass `bash -n`; every `git clone` retries + verifies `<dst>/.git` after; `--branch "$t"` preserved on each (check 71 green); `just drift-gate` green; stage only the Containerfile.

## AGY-286  (WS-BAKE, **P2**) — Firecrawl `pnpm install` retry + frozen-lockfile degrade  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Containerfile / bash). **When:** after AGY-285 (same firecrawl stanza).
**What + How:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` L113-116 runs `npm install -g pnpm@latest` then `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` then `pnpm run build:nosentry` — all single-shot. Two failure modes: (1) the registry install is un-retried (transient npm/pnpm registry flake aborts the bake); (2) `--frozen-lockfile` HARD-fails when the floated firecrawl release tag's committed `pnpm-lock.yaml` doesn't match `package.json` (a real risk since the tag floats latest per ADR-0003). Wrap `npm install -g pnpm@latest` and `pnpm install` in a 3×-backoff retry; and on a `--frozen-lockfile` failure, **degrade to a warn-and-retry without `--frozen-lockfile`** (`pnpm install --frozen-lockfile || { echo '[sys-bake] firecrawl lockfile drift -- retrying unfrozen'; pnpm install; }`) so a benign upstream lockfile bump doesn't block the whole bake, while still preferring the reproducible frozen path first. `pnpm run build:nosentry` stays fatal (a build failure is a real defect). Keep `ENV PUPPETEER_SKIP_DOWNLOAD=true` (L109) so no browser download sneaks in.
**Where:** `usr/share/mios/sys/Containerfile` (L110-116 firecrawl stanza).
**Done When:** extracted loop passes `bash -n`; pnpm registry ops retry; `--frozen-lockfile` degrades to unfrozen-with-warning on failure while `build:nosentry` stays fatal; `just drift-gate` green; stage only the Containerfile.

## AGY-287  (WS-BAKE, **P2**) — Consolidated static "bake network-op discipline" test + negative fixture  **[DONE]**

**Who:** you (bash + drift-gate). **What+How:** `automation/38-drift-checks.sh` defines `check_greenboot()` TWICE — the first at line 3120 (verifies `46-greenboot.sh` symlink commands + `etc/greenboot/*.sh` exec bits) and a second, unrelated `check_greenboot()` at line 3981 (health-coverage over `usr/lib/greenboot/check/required.d`) — so under bash the LATER definition wins and `main()`'s `check_greenboot` call (line 4266) runs ONLY the 3981 body; the 3120 exec-bit/symlink logic is silently DEAD. Rename the 3120 function (e.g. `check_greenboot_scripts`) and register it in `main()` so its symlink/exec-bit assertions run again, and rewrite the 3981 check to resolve `critical_services` from `[greenboot].critical_services` via `python3 -c tomllib` (degrade-open with a WARN when python3/tomllib absent, mirroring `check_cephfs_ssot` at line 3036) instead of the hardcoded array. **Where:** automation/38-drift-checks.sh (functions at 3120 + 3981, `main()` at ~4266), usr/share/mios/mios.toml `[greenboot]`, usr/lib/greenboot/check/required.d/. **Done When:** both greenboot checks run from `main()` (no shadowed/dead definition — `grep -c 'check_greenboot()' ` reflects the rename); the health-coverage check reads the SSOT list (adding a 4th service to `[greenboot].critical_services` with no matching required.d script makes it red); a negative-test in tests/drift-gate-negatives.sh proves it bites; `bash -n automation/38-drift-checks.sh` clean; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-303  (WS-RUNTIME, **P1**) — clevis/LUKS: collapse the DUAL disk-encryption SSOT tables into one canonical block  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash/Python + TOML + drift-gate). **What+How:** LUKS/clevis is configured from TWO disjoint, conflicting SSOT tables. `usr/libexec/mios/mios-clevis-luks-gen` (lines 7-9) reads `[security.luks]` (`enabled`/`pin`/`pcrs`, mios.toml:8658, where `pcrs="7"` is a STRING), while `usr/libexec/mios/mios-luks-enroll` — the script actually run by `mios-luks-enroll.service` (enabled in the preset, line 70) — reads a DIFFERENT table `[security.disk_encryption]` (`mode`/`backend`/`pcrs`/`recovery`, mios.toml:1083, where `pcrs=[7]` is a LIST). Nothing consumes `mios-clevis-luks-gen`'s output, so it is an orphaned generator whose SSOT can silently disagree with the enrollment path's SSOT (enabled=false vs mode="tpm2" right now). Pick `[security.disk_encryption]` as the ONE canonical table (it is the richer one the live service already reads), fold the `pin` concept into it (e.g. `pin`/`backend` reconciliation), repoint `mios-clevis-luks-gen` to read `[security.disk_encryption]` (deriving `CLEVIS_LUKS_ENABLED` from `mode!="none"`), and either delete `[security.luks]` or make it an alias the resolver rejects. Keep both surfaces shipping INERT by default (mode drives it). **Where:** usr/share/mios/mios.toml (`[security.luks]` 8658 + `[security.disk_encryption]` 1083), usr/libexec/mios/mios-clevis-luks-gen, usr/libexec/mios/mios-luks-enroll, automation/38-drift-checks.sh. **Done When:** one canonical disk-encryption table remains; `mios-clevis-luks-gen` and `mios-luks-enroll` resolve the SAME table (grep proves neither reads a second table); the `pcrs` type is consistent across both consumers; a drift-check asserts no orphaned second table reappears; `python3 -c 'import ast; ...'`/`bash -n` clean on both scripts; `just drift-gate` green.

## AGY-305  (WS-RUNTIME, **P1**) — ROCm/AMD: `[gpu.rocm]` SSOT + a static AMD CDI fallback generator (kfd+dri) when `amd-ctk` is absent  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (bash + TOML). **What+How:** `usr/lib/systemd/system/mios-gpu-amd.service` only emits a CDI spec `if command -v amd-ctk` (lines 21-23) — and `amd-ctk` is NOT in Fedora RPM repos (the unit's own comment says so), so ROCm containers get NO `/run/cdi/amd.json` and must hand-pass `--device /dev/kfd --device /dev/dri` forever, while NVIDIA has a full CDI plane (`usr/libexec/mios/mios-cdi-detect` + `nvidia-cdi-refresh` + greenboot `wanted.d/30-nvidia-cdi.sh`). The ROCm userland is already installed (`rocm-opencl`/`rocm-hip`/`rocm-runtime`/`rocm-smi`/`rocminfo` at mios.toml:7217) but there is NO `[gpu.rocm]` SSOT block. Add a `[gpu.rocm]` table (`enabled`, `render_group`, `devices` list defaulting to `/dev/kfd` + `/dev/dri/renderD*`, `cdi_kind = "amd.com/gpu"`) adjacent to `[gpu]` (mios.toml:675), and add a generator `usr/libexec/mios/mios-rocm-cdi-gen` that emits a deterministic static `/run/cdi/amd.json` (CDI v0.6 schema: one device enumerating the kfd+dri nodes with the render group) projected from `[gpu.rocm]`, used by `mios-gpu-amd.service` as the fallback when `amd-ctk` is absent. Ship INERT (`enabled=false` -> generator no-ops) so it is a pure no-op on non-AMD hosts. **Where:** new usr/libexec/mios/mios-rocm-cdi-gen, usr/lib/systemd/system/mios-gpu-amd.service (call the fallback), usr/share/mios/mios.toml (`[gpu.rocm]`). **Done When:** `mios-rocm-cdi-gen /path/to/mios.toml` emits schema-valid CDI JSON (`python3 -c 'import json,sys; json.load(...)'`) enumerating the kfd+dri devices from SSOT and no-ops when `[gpu.rocm].enabled=false`; the service references the generator in its non-`amd-ctk` branch; `bash -n` clean on both; no live GPU required.

## AGY-334 [DONE] (WS-DB, **P2**) — mios-userdb-render: project the account table into systemd userdb JSON drop-ins (Linux)  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python, systemd userdb / NSS). **What+How:** implement the doc §3(a) recommendation in `docs/agy/doc-postgresos-accounts.md` — a NEW `usr/libexec/mios/mios-userdb-render` (Python, same `get_pg_config()` + psycopg + `MIOS_TOML` gate style as `usr/libexec/mios/materialize-user-config.py`). It SELECTs enabled Linux-scoped rows from `account` (`WHERE enabled AND os_targets IN ('both','linux')`, same predicate as `mios-account-sync`'s `query_db_accounts`) and writes one JSON User Record per user to `/run/userdb/<name>.user` (runtime tmpfs, since the DB is the runtime SSOT and `/usr` is immutable) following systemd.io/USER_RECORD (`userName`, `uid`, `gid`, `realName`←display, `homeDirectory`←home_dir, `shell`, `disposition:"regular"`; hashed secret in a `.user-privileged` companion, mode 0600, NEVER plaintext). Idempotent: only rewrite when content changed (mirror materialize-user-config's compare-before-write at `:160-176`). Gate the whole run on `MIOS_ACCOUNTS_DB_BACKED` like `automation/17-accounts-db.sh`; fail-open (log + exit 0) when psycopg/DB is absent so offline boot is unaffected. This is a PROJECTION (bake/sync), keeping Postgres out of the login critical path per the doc's libnss-pgsql caution.
**Where:** usr/libexec/mios/mios-userdb-render. **Done When:** `python -m py_compile usr/libexec/mios/mios-userdb-render` passes; a dry-run with a mocked cursor (fixture rows) emits a valid User Record JSON that `python -c "import json;json.load(...)"` accepts and whose `userName`/`uid`/`homeDirectory` match the fixture; running twice on unchanged input writes nothing the second pass.

## AGY-340 [DONE] (WS-DB, **P3**) — Use the DB-owned uid_alloc sequence instead of the hardcoded 1000 gid fallback in account sync  **[DONE]**
**Who:** you (Python + SQL). **What+How:** `schema-init.sql:795-807` defines `SEQUENCE uid_alloc` + `allocate_uid()`/`allocate_gid()` precisely so ids are DB-owned, but `mios-account-sync:180` still falls back to a hardcoded `gid = db_user["gid"] or 1000` (and `useradd` at `:214-228` inherits it), which silently collides with the allocator and violates the everything-db-driven V4 intent. When a row has NULL uid/gid, allocate via the DB (`SELECT allocate_gid()` / `allocate_uid()` through `mios-pg-query`) and persist it back onto the `account` row (UPDATE), so ids are assigned ONCE in the DB and every projection (Linux useradd, AGY-334 userdb, AGY-339 Windows manifest) reads the same value. Keep the change additive and idempotent (only allocate when the column is NULL). Also drop the literal `1000` fallback from `query_db_accounts`'s default (`:180`) — an unresolved id is an error to log, not a magic default (NO-HARDCODE spirit).
**Where:** usr/libexec/mios/mios-account-sync. **Done When:** `python -m py_compile` passes; a stubbed-`mios-pg-query` unit test shows a NULL-gid row triggers exactly one `allocate_gid()` + one write-back UPDATE and reuses the stored id on the next pass; no `1000` literal remains in the id path; `just drift-gate` green.
